  - **What:** `MY_MOH.url_native = https://www.moh.gov.my/` (`utils/localeHint.ts`) is the official Malaysia MOH domain and the structural sibling of the HTTP-200-confirmed `moh.gov.sg`, but it **403s both `curl` (browser UA) and WebFetch** — a WAF bot-block, not a dead site. It is **BROWSER-CONFIRMED** live by the founder at b1 prod-gate row 2 (2026-07-26); it is **TOOL-UNCONFIRMED** and will stay that way.
  - **Why it's debt (the cron doesn't exist yet — this is a note for when it's built):** PRD §5.1.1 specifies a **monthly cron doing HEAD checks on every `url_native`**. A naive implementation flags `moh.gov.my` broken on every single run. One permanently-crying URL is worse than no monitor: the alert gets muted, and then a *genuinely* dead authority link (the exact failure the cron exists to catch — a regulator reorganising its domain, which is the normal way these links rot) goes unnoticed inside the noise. Link-health for the 在地差異 panel is a **credibility** surface: the panel's entire claim is "here is the official authority to verify against".
  - **Fix (when the cron is built, not before):** carry a per-URL `bot_blocked: true` / allowlist flag in the authority data (or a cron-side exception list) that maps 403-from-a-known-WAF to a distinct **"unverifiable-by-robot, human-recheck quarterly"** state, NOT to "dead". Do **not** silently treat all 403s as OK — that would hide real 403-serving breakage on other URLs.
  - **Related:** inline comment at the `MY_MOH` data entry in `utils/localeHint.ts` records the same TOOL-UNCONFIRMED / BROWSER-CONFIRMED status.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-26 (b1 pre-deploy edits).

- [DONE] **[P1 · Research golden R15 known-fail — surfaced by the K-union gate 2026-07-24 (fly 211), founder-accepted baseline amendment] Golden R15 (HIT Type-1/Type-2 distinction) is a STABLE FAIL = a pre-existing PubMed recall gap / live-API drift on a NON-DailyMed query; §2.7 Research floor recorded as 17/2/1-with-R15-known-fail until resolved**
  - **⚠️ RE-CHARACTERIZED 2026-07-27 (pair-aware probe, `docs/pair_aware_retrieval_probe.md` §6.1) — R15 IS NOT A STABLE FAIL. Read this bullet before the historical ones below.** Measured on HEAD, **N=3 → PASS / PASS / PASS**, with **zero intervening `api/` change** since fly 211 (b1 and b1-fix were frontend-only; verified zero `api/` in both merges). Timeline: **2026-07-21 PASS → 2026-07-24 FAIL ×3 → 2026-07-27 PASS ×3**, all on functionally identical retrieval code.
    - **This resolves an internal contradiction in the original entry**, which asserted both *"Confirmed STABLE on re-run (not judge oscillation)"* AND *"Live-API result variance + `_rewrite_query` nondeterminism → R15 flipped PASS→FAIL … with no code change"*. Those cannot both hold. The evidence now favours the second: the 2026-07-24 "stable" observation was **stable within one session**, which is not the same as stable across sessions.
    - **CLASS = `rewrite-nondeterminism pool churn` (deliberately NOT "oscillator").** In this repo "oscillator" (V12, R16/R20) means **judge-phrasing instability**: the same retrieved pool is scored differently run-to-run by the LLM judge. **R15's mechanism is different**: the retrieved **pool itself changes**, so the generator answers from genuinely different documents. Same symptom (verdict moves without a code change), different cause, different remedy — a judge-rubric change would address an oscillator and do nothing for R15.
      *(⚠️ This bullet first proposed the label `live-index drift`, defined as external PubMed drift on a multi-day timescale. **That was wrong** — see the renamed class below: the driver is our own `_rewrite_query` nondeterminism, intra-session, and external index drift remains unobserved. Wording kept for provenance only.)*
    - **⚠️ N=3 WITHIN ONE SESSION — establishes "not a stable fail", does NOT establish a drift period.** Three consecutive PASSes minutes apart say nothing about how often, or on what cycle, the pool changes. The drift period is **unmeasured**. To characterize it, a future effort would need R15 sampled on a schedule (e.g. daily N=3 for 2–3 weeks) **with the retrieved PMID set recorded per run** — the pool identity, not just the verdict, is the signal. Not scoped here.
  - **What:** golden R15 ("What is the difference between Type 1 and Type 2 heparin-induced thrombocytopenia?") FAILs the LLM-judge **when it fails** because the answer honestly states *"the provided context only discusses type II … does not provide information about type I HIT"* — the Type-1/non-immune/mild-platelet-drop concept is **absent from the retrieved pool on those runs**. A retrieval RECALL gap, not a generation error (the generator correctly refuses to fabricate). *(Original wording said "Confirmed STABLE on re-run (not judge oscillation)" — the STABLE half is superseded by the 2026-07-27 measurement above; the "not judge oscillation" half still holds and is now sharpened to `live-index drift`.)*
  - **Provably NOT lever-induced (K-union/cut-exemption orthogonal), two counterfactuals:** (1) **pool** — lever-ON vs lever-OFF (in-process) return the IDENTICAL top-3 PubMed docs (PMID 11235727 / 16798180 / 17174213), **zero DailyMed in the pool either way**; (2) **verdict** — R15 ×3 on ACTUAL pre-lever code (`0b05de5:api/rag/retriever.py` swapped in, source-weight + surface-(iii) retained) through the real golden judge = **0 PASS / 1 WARN / 2 FAIL**, all 3 missing the Type-1 concept. R15 never passes without the levers → the FAIL reproduces on pre-lever code. The levers never touch this query (it's PubMed-only, no DailyMed).
  - **Cause (real):** PubMed live-API for this query returns Type-II-focused HIT articles; no retrieved doc carries the Type-1/non-immune distinction. Live-API result variance + `_rewrite_query` nondeterminism → R15 flipped PASS→FAIL vs the 2026-07-21 baseline with no code change touching its path.
  - **~~Founder-accepted baseline amendment (2026-07-24): the §2.7 Research floor is 17 PASS / 2 WARN / 1 FAIL with R15 a known, accepted fail~~ — ⚠️ SUPERSEDED 2026-07-27. That rule is a HOLE and must not be used.**
    - **Why it is unsafe:** "17/2/1 with R15 pre-accepted" lets **any** future gate that lands on 17/2/1 be explained away as *"that's just R15"* — while the single FAIL is actually a **different, genuinely regressed case**. The rule makes a real regression and the known issue produce an identical, passing-looking signature. It also mis-files R15 with the R16/R20 **oscillator** acceptances, which are a different class (see the re-characterization above).
    - **✅ REPLACEMENT RULE (proposed 2026-07-27, founder to ratify): R15 is NOT pre-accepted. Evaluate it on its own merits at every gate, BY NAME.**
      - The Research floor is **18 PASS / 2 WARN / 0 FAIL**, and **every** FAIL — including R15 — must be **named and adjudicated individually**. A gate result is never satisfied by a count alone.
      - **If R15 is the failing case:** it does **not** block the ship *provided* the `live-index drift` signature is confirmed for that run (see the escalation condition below). Record the verdict **and** the retrieved PMID set in the gate report. If the drift signature is **not** confirmed, treat it as a normal blocking FAIL.
      - **If any case OTHER than R15 fails:** it blocks, exactly as before. R15's status confers nothing on any other case.
      - **If R15 PASSes** (as on 2026-07-27) the floor is met at 18/2/0 with no exemption consumed — do not record a "known fail" that did not occur.
  - **⚠️ ESCALATION CONDITION — REWRITTEN 2026-07-27. 🔴 THE SINGLE-RUN POOL-COMPARISON IN THIS BULLET IS ITSELF WITHDRAWN — see "ESCALATION DISCRIMINATOR" below for the rule actually in force.** *(Retained for provenance: it correctly identified that "escalate on ANY movement" is unusable, but its replacement test was wrong.)* The original text (*"investigate ANY R15 movement as a real regression"*) is a **false-alarm generator**: R15 now demonstrably moves **on its own**, with PubMed, with no code change at all. Investigating every movement would burn a gate cycle on external drift. The condition must **discriminate by evidence, not by movement**:
    - **Capture at every gate:** the **retrieved PMID set** for R15 (not just the verdict). This is the discriminator and it is cheap.
    - **`live-index drift` (do NOT escalate):** the verdict moved **and** the retrieved pool **changed identity** (different PMIDs vs the last recorded set). The generator answered from a different corpus; nothing in our code caused it. Record the new PMID set and move on.
    - **🔴 CODE-CAUSED (DO escalate — investigate as a real regression):** the verdict moved **while the retrieved pool is IDENTICAL** (same PMIDs as last recorded) → the change is in *our* ranking/filtering/generation, not in PubMed. This is the signature the original condition was trying to catch.
    - **🔴 ALSO escalate:** the pool changed identity **in a way attributable to the diff under test** (e.g. the change touched the rewrite prompt, PubMed source, reranker, relevance filter, or threshold, and the pool shift correlates with lever ON/OFF in-process). The fly-211 gate's own two-counterfactual method (pool identity + verdict on pre-lever code) is the template.
    - **Baseline PMID set (2026-07-24, lever ON and OFF identical):** `11235727` / `16798180` / `17174213`.
  - **✅ POOL-IDENTITY CAPTURE NOW EXISTS (2026-07-27, tests-only).** `tests/run_golden_tests.py` records, per case per run, the retrieved pool identity + which citation ids the answer references, under a new additive `pool_identity` key in `tests/results/golden_results_*.json`. Captured from the `citations` SSE event (`api/server.py:903`), which `api/rag/generator.py:179` builds from **every** document handed to the generator — so it is the **final top_k pool in retrieval order**, not an LLM-selected subset. *(Scope limit: the pre-top_k candidate pool is not exposed over SSE and would require an `api/` change to capture.)*
  - **📌 FIRST MEASURED BASELINE — R15, 2026-07-27, N=3, all PASS:**

    | Run | pool_size | PMIDs (retrieval order) |
    |---|---|---|
    | 1 | **1** | `8975463` |
    | 2 | **5** | `9475822` · `27230048` · `32337112` · `20059332` · `37601013` |
    | 3 | **1** | `8975463` |

  - **📌 SECOND BASELINE — R15, 2026-07-28, N=3: `[8975463]` ×3, turnover 0.0 (perfectly stable).** ⚠️ **The day before it was fully disjoint** (`[8975463]` / 5-PMID set / `[8975463]`, turnover 1.0). **A single day's N=3 can look stable and still be a churning query** — so a stable-looking arm is NOT evidence of stability, and any causal test must compare distributions *across versions*, never a single arm against intuition. Full corpus-wide context: `docs/poolsize_distribution_harvest.md`.
  - **🔴 THIS BASELINE CONTRADICTS PART OF THE DISCRIMINATOR WRITTEN ABOVE — correcting it rather than leaving it unexecutable (Rule 18).**
    - **Measured fact:** the retrieved pool changes identity **BETWEEN CONSECUTIVE RUNS MINUTES APART** — three runs in ~4 minutes produced two entirely disjoint pools and a 5× swing in pool size. The live PubMed index did not change in those 4 minutes; the upstream **`_rewrite_query` nondeterminism** (already documented: 3–6 distinct rewrite-sets per 12 runs, 2026-07-23 probe) issues different PubMed queries, which return different documents.
    - **Consequence:** the rule *"verdict moved + pool IDENTICAL → code-caused, escalate"* is **near-unexecutable as a single-run comparison**, because the pool is almost never identical even with zero code change. Applied naively it would classify essentially everything as drift — the same lenient failure the rewrite was meant to prevent.
    - **✅ CORRECTED DISCRIMINATOR — compare DISTRIBUTIONS over N runs, never single runs:**
      - Run R15 **N≥3** at each gate and record all N pools (the harness now does this automatically).
      - **`drift` (do NOT escalate):** the **union of observed PMIDs turns over** — PMIDs never seen in prior baselines dominate, and previously-seen ones stop appearing. The candidate population itself moved.
      - **🔴 CODE-CAUSED (DO escalate):** the **PMID union is stable** versus the recorded baseline, but *selection or ordering within it* shifts systematically (e.g. the same PMIDs are retrieved yet consistently ranked out of top_k, or pool_size collapses across all N runs). The population is the same; our handling of it changed.
      - **🔴 ALSO escalate:** the union shift correlates with the diff under test in-process (lever ON/OFF), per the fly-211 two-counterfactual template.
    - **✅ `pool_size` instability now QUANTIFIED corpus-wide (2026-07-28, 25 cases × N=3 = 75 runs, 0 excluded — `docs/poolsize_distribution_harvest.md`):** distribution `{0:1, 1:6, 2:1, 3:5, 4:18, 5:44}` → **`pool_size ≤ 2` on 11% of runs, clustered in 3/25 cases (R02, R15, TB04)**, not spread thinly. **Churn is the norm:** mean union-turnover **0.423**, median 0.333; only **7/25** cases fully stable, **2/25** (R02, R17) fully disjoint across three runs on identical input. Whether a 1-doc pool is *healthy* remains **unassessed** — it may belong with the `_filter_by_relevance` [P2] entry.
    - **🔴 `RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` CANNOT SEE THE SMALL-POOL CASES — its dataset is censored against exactly the shape it exists to detect (read-only code check, 2026-07-28).** `api/server.py:571-572` returns early when `len(sources) < 2`, where `sources` comes from `pool_sources_from_documents` (`api/services/retrieval_refusal.py:69-79`), which counts **PubMed documents only** (`:73-74` skips every non-PubMed source_type) that also carry a PMID and non-empty content (`:77`). Consequences: **a `pool_size=1` pool can never reach `rr.assess()`**; and the gate is on **PubMed-doc count, not pool size**, so a 2-doc pool of 1 PubMed + 1 DailyMed is also short-circuited. **Same class as the already-recorded "SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW dataset is CENSORED" entry** — a shadow whose sample non-randomly excludes the population of interest. **Nothing was changed or enabled.**
      - **Prod shadow logs may therefore already hold population-level evidence — but only a LOWER BOUND.** `RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` is ON in prod, so its records characterise pools with **≥2 PubMed docs** and are **structurally blind to the ≤1 case**. Reading them would understate small-pool frequency. **Not fetched this baton**, per scope.
  - **✅ CLASS RENAMED 2026-07-27 → `rewrite-nondeterminism pool churn` (founder decision; supersedes `live-index drift`, which was wrong).**
    - **The mechanism is OUR OWN CODE, intra-session — not an external index.** `_rewrite_query` is nondeterministic at the shipped `temperature=0` (3–6 distinct rewrite-sets per 12 runs, 2026-07-23 probe). Different rewrites → different PubMed queries → **different retrieved pools, minutes apart**. Measured directly: R15 N=3 in ~4 minutes produced two **entirely disjoint** pools and a 5× pool-size swing (baseline table above).
    - **🔴 EXTERNAL PubMed index drift is UNOBSERVED AND UNPROVEN. It must NOT be used as a default explanation at a gate.** The full `2026-07-21 PASS → 07-24 FAIL → 07-27 PASS` sequence is **completely explained by rewrite churn alone** — no second mechanism is required, and none has been demonstrated. An unproven mechanism invoked at a gate is exactly the excuse that reopens the lenient floor this entry closed. If someone wants to claim external drift, they must **evidence** it (e.g. the same rewrite string returning a different PMID set across days), not assume it.
    - **✅ THE FIX SHAPE IS ALREADY VALIDATED IN THIS REPO — and R15 sits precisely in the slice it was withheld from.** fly 211's **K=3 rewrite-union** (BACKLOG:789, `api/rag/retriever.py:174`/`:386`) exists to defeat exactly this churn: run the rewrite K× and union the pools so a good rewrite in the distribution always contributes. It was **deliberately scoped DailyMed-only** — *"non-DailyMed sources stay K=1, no pool inflation"* — and **R15 is a PubMed-only query** (zero DailyMed in its pool, per the 2026-07-24 counterfactual). So the one query class that most visibly suffers churn is in the excluded slice, by design.
    - **📌 RECORDED, NOT SCOPED — extending K-union to PubMed is a CANDIDATE, not a plan.** It has a validated shape but carries an **explicit prior tradeoff that was the stated reason for the DailyMed-only scoping: pool inflation.** PubMed already contributes the bulk of candidates; unioning K=3 PubMed rewrites would enlarge the candidate pool on **every** Research query, with knock-on effects on the relevance filter, the reranker, cost, and latency. **It needs its own measurement — do not assume the DailyMed result transfers.**
  - **⚠️ ESCALATION DISCRIMINATOR — USE THIS PROJECT'S EXISTING CAUSAL TEST, NOT A NEW ONE (corrected 2026-07-27).** My earlier single-version pool-identity comparison was **wrong and is withdrawn**: with churn this large, pools differ run-to-run under *zero* code change, so inspecting one version's pools **cannot establish causation** in either direction.
    - **✅ The test is: run the SAME case N≥3 on BOTH the pre-change and post-change code, in-process, and compare the two DISTRIBUTIONS.** Only a difference between versions is evidence about the change.
    - **Precedent in this repo (cite it, don't reinvent):** the **fly-205 A2-typo baton** reverted `34067-9` → `34067-0` and re-ran **V07 on the pre-fix code** to prove the WARN was judge oscillation rather than indications-caused (STATE fly-205 entry). The **fly-211 gate** used the same two-arm shape for R15 (pool identity + verdict on real pre-lever code `0b05de5`). Both are the pattern to follow.
    - **What the `pool_identity` capture is FOR:** it supplies the per-run pool evidence each arm needs. It is **not**, on its own, a causation test.
  - **Repair (separate, unscoped):** the Type-1/2 HIT distinction is a general-medical fact absent from live PubMed's top results for this phrasing — closing it is a retrieval-recall / query-rewrite improvement with Research-wide blast radius (own §2.7), NOT bundled here.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-24 (K-union gate). **Evidence:** `tests/results/kunion_gate_report.md` §5 · gate JSON `kunion_gate_20260724_074839/075000.json`.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · deploy tooling — surfaced during the fly-211 deploy 2026-07-24] `deploy.ps1` stopped-machine parser (`^\S+`) skips fly's indented `fly status` rows → the auto-restart step is a no-op; the script claims a step it does not perform**
  - **What:** `deploy.ps1` Step 3 extracts stopped-machine IDs with `if ($_ -match "^\S+\s+(\S+)")` (line ~22), but `fly status` rows begin with **leading whitespace** (` app │ …`), so `^\S+` never matches → `$stopped` is always empty → the script prints "All machines running" and **skips Steps 4–6 (start stopped machines)** even when a machine is `stopped`.
  - **Observed (fly 211):** machine `2879720c66d478` was `stopped` (autostop baseline, on v211, fly "good state"); the script reported "All machines running" without restarting it. **Harmless** because Fly autostop wakes the machine on the first inbound request, so prod is reachable regardless.
  - **Why it's still debt:** the script's stated purpose ("auto-restart any stopped machines after deployment", per CLAUDE.md) is **not actually performed** — a Rule-18 "claims a step it doesn't do" mismatch. If a machine is ever stopped for a NON-autostart reason (crash-loop, OOM), the script would silently leave it down while reporting success.
  - **Fix (small):** parse the machine ID from the whitespace-prefixed row (e.g. `-match "\bapp\b\s+(\S+)"` or split on `│`), or use `fly machines list --json` and filter `state == "stopped"`. Behavior with autostop is unchanged; the fix restores the honest auto-restart for the non-autostart case.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-24 (fly 211 deploy).

- [OTHER] **[P2 · relevance-filter quality — surfaced by the filter-exemption probe 2026-07-20, `tests/results/filterexempt_probe_REPORT.md`] `_filter_by_relevance` (gpt-4.1-mini) keeps OFF-TOPIC docs while dropping the query drug's own safety section — MASKED (not fixed) for whitelisted safety sections by the fly-209 exemption**
  - **What:** the Research relevance filter is **deterministic per identical input but input-brittle** across near-identical candidate pools (keeps 2 / 4 / 5 docs on 冠脂妥+warfarin runs). When it minimizes to keep-2 it takes the top-2 by RETRIEVAL score and **keeps a clearly-off-topic doc** — measured: a **Fostamatinib pharmacokinetics study (score 0.85)** kept while the drug's own **rosuvastatin Drug-Interactions section (#34073-7 @ 0.6265) is dropped**, on a rosuvastatin+warfarin query. Not randomness (Part-B determinism 8/8 identical), not a fixed count.
  - **Masked, not fixed:** the fly-209 exemption (`65da848`, surface iii) re-adds dropped **whitelisted SAFETY sections** (interactions/contra/warnings/boxed) only. **Non-whitelisted relevance errors persist** — the filter can still drop a relevant PubMed study or a descriptive label section, or keep an off-topic study, on ANY Research query.
  - **Repair = separate future candidate:** fixing the filter's judgment (e.g. reject off-topic high-retrieval-score docs / floor the keep, or tighten the prompt) is an **LLM-judgment change with blast radius = EVERY Research query** → needs a full §2.7 pass, not just danger-path. Do NOT bundle with a targeted exemption.
  - **Model note (docs-fix candidate, do not fix here):** `Cost_Analysis_v2` lists the relevance_filter model as **4o-mini** — STALE. It is **gpt-4.1-mini** (`get_lightweight_provider()`, factory default, no `LIGHTWEIGHT_MODEL` override); architecture.md is correct. Update the cost doc when convenient.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-20 (probe). **Masked:** 2026-07-21 (fly 209).

- [DONE] **[P1 · retrieval-ranking / silent-inert — surfaced 2026-07-14, ROOT-CAUSED by the probe 2026-07-16 — ✅ RESOLVED: SLICE 1 (observability) `201e5eb`/fly 207 + SLICE 2 (keyed-schema output-contract fix) `4319884`/fly 208 (2026-07-17)] Reranker rerank-skip silently BYPASSED the v200 source-weighting composite on ~32% of Research queries**
  - **Mechanism (probe 2026-07-16, `tests/results/reranker_instability_probe_REPORT.md`):** the reject at `api/rag/reranker.py:130` is a **DETERMINISTIC off-by-one COUNT MISMATCH**, NOT a malformed format and NOT stochastic instability. gpt-4o-mini returns **clean, valid JSON with exactly `len(documents) − 1` scores** (`json.loads` succeeds; `finish_reason=stop`, ~15–30 out-tokens ≪ the 200 cap, so NOT truncation), which trips `len(scores) != len(documents)`. It is a function of **candidate-pool size**: **0%** fail at ≤4 docs, **4%** at 5, **88–100%** at **≥6** (temp=0, reproducible 3/3 on identical doc lists). Measured overall **31.9% = 30/94** LLM-issued rerank calls; **all 30 failures were delta=1** (zero non-list, zero other deltas).
  - **NOT Baton-A related (hypothesis REFUTED two ways):** (1) the reranker truncates every doc to **400 chars** (`reranker.py:76`), so DailyMed's un-truncated tables (up to ~20k chars) never reach the LLM; (2) DailyMed-in-pool fail rate **30%** ≈ no-DailyMed **33%**. CJK / long-prompt correlations are confounds of pool size, not independent drivers. The ~32% is **pre-existing**, exposed whenever the relevance filter keeps ≥6 candidates (common: `candidates[:20]`, `top_k=8`).
  - **Impact:** on a rerank-skip, `_score_sink` stays empty, so **BOTH** the shadow-extend gate (`retriever.py:199`) **AND** the v200 activation gate (`retriever.py:208`) — which share the `and _score_sink` condition — are bypassed. The composite (rerank × source_weight × tier_weight) does NOT apply → `SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE=ON` is **silently inert on ~32% of queries**. Pre-existing; affects **ALL** Research ranking, NOT DailyMed-specific. It also **manufactured the false "#5 prominence" signal** that triggered B-2 Phase 2b (the one-off methotrexate "#5" was a rerank-failed run; clean runs = **#0, 5/5**). *(Citation correction: the debt previously cited `retriever.py:199` as the activation gate; :199 is the **shadow-extend** gate, the **activation** gate is **:208** — both share the empty-sink condition, so the bypass conclusion holds.)*
  - **✅ SLICE 1 — observability, SHIPPED `201e5eb` (2026-07-16), behavior-neutral:** `[RERANK_SKIP reason=format_reject|exception]` WARNING with expected/received counts + model + raw payload (500-char cap); process-cumulative `_RERANK_STATS` counter + a `[RERANK_STATS] … inert_rate=%` summary line (Fly-log greppable, no DB/metrics/PostHog); `[SOURCE_WEIGHT_INERT]` WARNING at the empty-sink activation fall-through (`retriever.py:208`). Ordering / acceptance / outputs byte-identical (6 new tests in `tests/test_reranker_observability.py`; source_weight identity/parity/shadow 30/30 green).
  - **✅ SLICE 2 — the fix, SHIPPED `4319884` / fly 208 (2026-07-17): keyed-by-index output contract.** The reranker now emits `{"scores":[{"index":i,"score":n},...]}` with `response_format={"type":"json_object"}` (SDK 1.30.1 — no Structured-Outputs helper, not upgraded); score attaches to its INDEX (structural alignment) → a dropped element is a detectable missing index, not an off-by-one length trip. Reject-on-missing (no fabrication — partial-accept is a scoring-semantics change, out of scope): reasons `keyed_missing` / `keyed_invalid` (dup / out-of-range / bool-index / non-numeric / positional-array) / `json_parse`. **Exception path now BYPASSES** (empty sink → composite bypassed + relevance order), replacing the pre-slice-2 stale-score activation (which ranked on ERASED pre-rerank scores). `[RERANK_STATS]` upgraded INFO→WARNING (ships through Fly log loss). `max_tokens=min(4096,40n+200)`; rubric bands / doc formatting / temp=0 unchanged.
    - **SCOPE (founder-reframed — this is a RERANKER OUTPUT-CONTRACT CHANGE affecting ordering across ALL pool sizes, NOT merely a ≥6 coverage fix):** ≥6 pools (previously skipped ~88–100%) flip relevance-order → composite (8/9..12/12 of measured ≥6 queries change top-3); **≤5 pools (already reranked) ALSO reorder** — the keyed prompt scores docs somewhat differently than the old positional prompt → **9/22** measured ≤5 queries change top-3 (score rank-corr **0.83**; **6/9 order-only** reshuffles of the same cited sources + **3/9 single-source boundary swaps** between comparable evidence), vs a **1/24 old-vs-old nondeterminism floor** (so it is a real schema effect, not noise). Founder-accepted 2026-07-17.
    - **Gates (local, deployed commit):** offline parse-success **100%** at pool 4/5/6/8/11/13/16/20; **§2.7 Research 18 PASS / 2 WARN / 0 FAIL** at floor (**R20 oscillator CONFIRMED — WARN/WARN/PASS across 3 runs**, since this diff changes ordering the "zero prompt contact" R20 exemption did not apply automatically; R16 standing WARN); **section-aware danger-path 0 violations / 0 rechecks** (the new reranker now surfaces DailyMed *safety* sections on danger queries — Drug Interactions on 冠脂妥+warfarin, Contraindications on 冠脂妥懷孕); **prominence held** (DailyMed safety #0–#1 on the newly-covered slice, `[0×11,1,1]`, **NO weight change**); cost ~1.9× (~+$0.00009/query). **Post-deploy prod log-grep:** `RERANK_SKIP` 0, `SOURCE_WEIGHT_INERT` 0, `[RERANK_STATS] inert_rate=0.0%` (was ~32%), a 9-doc pool reranked successfully. Offline artifacts (gitignored): `slice2_offline_*` / `slice2_offline_v3_*` (controlled old-vs-new) / `slice2_control_*` (old-vs-old floor). **v2→v3 harness note:** v1/v2 used relevance-order as the OLD baseline for ALL pools (conflating rerank-vs-no-rerank on ≤5) → inflated "27/31"; v3 fixed it with a single retrieval + deepcopy so old-positional and new-keyed score IDENTICAL docs (the reportable 9/22 ≤5 + 8/9 ≥6 come from v3; the 12/12 in v2 is the same story on that run's pool split).
    - **Discovered:** 2026-07-14 (Phase 2b, 3/11). **Root-caused:** 2026-07-16 (probe). **Fixed:** 2026-07-17 (slice 2).

- [OTHER] **[P1 · measurement-integrity — surfaced by the reranker probe 2026-07-16] `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` dataset is CENSORED — silently excludes the ~32% rerank-failed queries, non-randomly**
  - **What:** the shadow record is built only when `_sw_shadow_sink` is non-empty (`server.py:954`), and the sink is populated only via `_score_sink` (`retriever.py:199`), which the rerank-skip path leaves EMPTY (same root cause as the entry above). So every v198-onward `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` record **omits the queries where rerank skipped** — and it omits them **non-randomly**: exactly the **large-pool (≥6-doc)** queries, which are the ones most likely to contain a label-vs-study inversion the shadow exists to measure.
  - **Consequence:** any conclusion drawn from shadow data (e.g. "the composite rarely changes top-k", "×1.5 label weight is sufficient") is computed on a sample **biased toward small pools** and must carry this caveat until slice 2 lands. In particular the B-2 Phase-2b prominence result was measured on the surviving (rerank-succeeded) slice only.
  - **Resolution — DE-CENSORING NOW IN EFFECT as of fly 208 (2026-07-17, reranker slice 2 `4319884`):** the rerank-skip is fixed, so `_score_sink` now populates on the previously-failing ~32% (large-pool) queries and `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` records them for the first time. **⚠️ EXPECT the shadow's volume + composition to SHIFT** (more large pools, more label-vs-study inversions surfacing) — this is the sample **de-censoring**, NOT a regression. Do NOT read the pre-208 vs post-208 shadow delta as a behavior change. **Closure LEFT TO THE FOUNDER** until enough post-fix shadow data accumulates to characterize the de-censored distribution. Cross-ref `tests/results/reranker_instability_probe_REPORT.md` + the reranker [P1] entry above.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-16 (probe). **De-censoring in effect:** 2026-07-17 (fly 208).

- [OTHER] **[P2 · lever-verification method — surfaced v198, 2026-07-06] The lever-digest fingerprint is no longer unique across shadow flags**
  - **What:** `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW` (added v198) and `RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` both resolve to Fly digest **`d8c5ac2e11c8e492` (= `d8c5ac2e`)** because Fly hashes the secret **VALUE** (`'true'`), NOT the flag name — identical values → identical digest.
  - **Consequence:** the long-standing "`RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW` digest = `d8c5ac2e`" check can no longer be read as proof that RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL **alone** is unchanged — the same digest now covers two flags (and any future flag also set to `'true'` will collide too).
  - **FIX (method, not code):** every future lever-state verification must enumerate **EACH flag's state by name** (`RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW`, `SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW`, C1 / `DIRECTION_CHECK_SHADOW`, Lever 2 / `QUESTION_NEUTRALIZATION_SHADOW`, locale-hint), not compare a single shared digest. Update the deploy / post-deploy checklist wording accordingly.
  - **Not a bug in v198** — a verification-method blind spot introduced by a second shadow flag sharing a value.
  - **Surfaced:** 2026-07-06 (v198 review).
  - **🆕 SECOND-SURFACE wrinkle (fly 210, 2026-07-22 — locale-hint enable):** the `locale-hint` lever is **NOT a fly secret** — it is `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOCALE_HINT_ENABLED`, a **build-time build-arg** in committed `fly.toml [build.args]` (`= "true"`, commit `8499495`). It therefore **NEVER appears in `fly secrets list`** — its state is auditable ONLY in the committed `fly.toml` (and, at the bundle level, by whether `localeHintEnabled` constant-folds out of the research-chunk gate). **So future lever enumerations must check TWO surfaces:** (1) `fly secrets list` for the five secret-based flags (RETRIEVAL_REFUSAL_SHADOW / SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW / SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE / DIRECTION_CHECK_SHADOW / QUESTION_NEUTRALIZATION_SHADOW), AND (2) `fly.toml [build.args]` for `NEXT_PUBLIC_LOCALE_HINT_ENABLED`. Enumerating only `fly secrets list` would silently omit the locale-hint state entirely. Same class of blind spot as the shared-digest caveat above — a lever whose state lives on a surface the default check doesn't read.

- [DONE] **[P1 · citation-integrity — v193-origin, surfaced by the v196 deep-link — ✅ FIXED v197 (2026-07-03, `dd63bf2` script+test + `07dddd8` corpus/embedding regen)] grounding-lite corpus representative NAME and 許可證字號 were selected INDEPENDENTLY — citation card could show drug A's name with drug B's 字號**
  - **What:** the grounding-lite corpus build (v193) selected the representative NAME and the representative 許可證字號 **independently** per ingredient×indication group (`scripts/build_tfda_indication_corpus.py` — `rep_zh` = shortest product name, `rep_lic` = alphabetically-first license in the group), so within multi-license groups the citation's 代表品名 and 字號 could come from **different rows**. Example: **冠脂妥膜衣錠5毫克 shown with 衛署藥製字第057803號 — which is 諾脂替膜衣錠20毫克's (Roty, 中國化學製藥) license**. The mismatch was in the displayed content since v193; **v196's deep-link made it clickable → visible** (card says 冠脂妥, link opened 諾脂替's 仿單資料 page).
  - **Blast radius (quantified 2026-07-03, read-only diagnostic):** **1,327 / 10,941 docs (12.1%)**, all inside the 2,193 multi-license groups; the 8,748 single-license docs were structurally immune.
  - **NOT medical-accuracy:** the group shares an identical ingredient set AND identical 適應症 text by construction, so the cited content (ingredient + indication) is correct for every 字號 in the group — this was a **citation-integrity / attribution + trust** defect.
  - **NOT shared with T2a/v195:** `brand_ingredient.json` is per-name row-true (冠脂妥 → its own 024129/024131/024597; 057803 absent) — Verify v191/v194 grounding and the Research v195 annotation were unaffected.
  - **NOT data-drift:** the pinned id=37 snapshot (2026-06-30) agrees with TFDA — 057803 = 諾脂替 in our own data.
  - **FIX (v197):** the build now captures a per-NAME license set and draws `rep_lic` from the representative NAME's OWN rows → title / 代表品名 / 字號 / deep-link are one coherent row (冠脂妥膜衣錠5毫克 now cites its own 衛署藥輸字第024597號). The +N grouping COUNT is preserved. **Post-fix mismatch count = 0** (was 1,327), pinned by `tests/test_tfda_corpus_urls.py::test_representative_name_and_license_are_one_row`. Selective re-embed: only the 1,327 changed-content docs re-embedded (text-embedding-3-small, unchanged model); the 8,748 single-license docs are byte-identical and their vectors bit-preserved. Danger-path re-verified (TFDA cited = 0 on 冠脂妥+warfarin AND 冠脂妥懷孕); indication control cites a self-consistent doc; §2.7 Research golden sanity recorded in STATE v197.
  - **Discovered:** 2026-07-03 (founder human-eye click-test on the v196 deep-link; root-caused same day). **Fixed:** 2026-07-03 (v197).

  - **What (probe-confirmed 2026-06-30):** the daily retention job (`api/server.py:190`) filtered `AuditLog.created_at`, which does NOT exist on the model — the column is **`timestamp`** (`sql_models.py:9`). Deployed v192; prod READ-ONLY count confirmed `audit_logs` 998 / `chat_history` 1276 both `>180d=0` before AND after deploy (data undisturbed). The daily loop (`sleep 86400`) first fires ~24h post-deploy.
  - **Discovered:** 2026-06-25 (v189 log review). **Probed + fixed:** 2026-06-30.

  - **⚠️ Probe-to-confirm the seam (CC, BEFORE building):** confirm whether the transparency string is actually in the `/api/verify` response payload. **IF yes** → pure frontend render fix (display-only, low-risk, no §2.7). **IF the backend does NOT emit it** → slightly more (backend surfaces resolved-INN + source into the payload, then frontend renders) — still small, touches the Verify result shape. Founder prod observation = "backend summary has it, frontend doesn't render" → most likely the former, but the payload check decides.
  - **Risk note:** rendering an already-computed deterministic mapping is a transparency DISCLOSURE, not a change to the medical analysis → not a generation/prompt change. Likely display-only pending the seam probe; still human-eye verify (Verify = medical-output surface).
  - **Cross-ref:** STATE v191 (T2a `7c50fd6`). The grounding-lite follow-ups (a1-i..a1-iv, BACKLOG) are the **v193 Research** family — this is the DISTINCT **v191 Verify** family, tracked separately here. Same citation/chip display layer as the DailyMed chip-label [P2] below.

- [OTHER] **[P2 · founder-decision — RATIFIED 2026-07-06 → ACTIVE ON PROD v200 → ⚰️ NOW DEAD CONFIG as of c1/fly 215 (2026-07-29)] Local drug-store evidence-tier default is UNSPECIFIED by the PRD and is load-bearing**
  - **⚰️ STATUS CORRECTION 2026-08-03 — this entry's header used to read "✅ ACTIVE ON PROD", which is no longer true and contradicted its own body.** `local` is deprecated and no longer retrieved (c1, fly 215), so **`SOURCE_WEIGHTS["local"] = 1.5` in `api/services/source_weight_shadow.py:31` can never fire** — it is **dead configuration, not an active weighting decision**. The tier question is therefore **moot unless the corpus is revived**. The PREMISE-REFUTED / REOPENED analysis below stands as the historical record of *why*; **no re-tiering decision is needed any more — only cleanup** (listed in the c1 build record's "dead-config cleanup"). Kept, not deleted, because the reasoning is cross-referenced by the instrument-blind class table.
  - **What:** the §2.10.6 5-tier classifier (`api/services/source_weight_shadow.py`) needs a tier for the LOCAL drug vector store, which the PRD §2.10.6 does NOT specify (it names PubMed / DailyMed / openFDA / WHO only). The shadow currently defaults **local = Tier 2** (treated as cached FDA drug labeling, same as openFDA; source_weight ×1.5), flagged in-code as `FOUNDER-FLAGGED`.
  - **Why it matters (data):** the v198 first data report shows this default is **load-bearing** — the ONLY substantive reweighting effect in the 13-query battery was 4 intra-top-5 inversions on 「冠脂妥懷孕」 that all promote a `local` cached-FDA-label doc (Tier 2 ×1.5 → multiplier 2.25) above four Tier-4 observational PubMed studies (×1.0). If local is retiered lower (e.g. Tier 4, or a <1.0 source_weight), that effect disappears and the shadow shows ~zero impact at spec weights. So the founder's tier choice for `local` directly determines whether source-weighting does anything at current pool sizes.
  - **Decision needed (✅ RESOLVED — ratified 2026-07-06, see ratification bullet below):** confirm local = Tier 2 (label-authority) OR set a different tier/source_weight. Also confirm the PubMed **"Review"** publication_type → Tier 4 (narrative/commentary) mapping — PubMed's bare "Review" tag is ambiguous (can include high-quality narrative reviews). Both are config-only (`SOURCE_WEIGHTS` / `TIER_WEIGHTS` / `_PUBTYPE_TIER`), no logic change.
  - **📊 SWEEP EVIDENCE + RECOMMENDATION (2026-07-06, ✅ ratified by founder 2026-07-06)** — 50-query battery (`scripts/source_weight_battery.json`, 10/category), local-tier swept T1..T5 + local-as-study + Review T4/T2 via config-only rebind of the v198 scorer (no logic change). Raw: `tests/results/local_tier_sweep_20260706_134420.json` + `local_tier_decision_report_20260706.md` (gitignored artifacts). 31/50 non-empty pools; local appears in 14.
    - **⚠️ DANGER-PATH INVARIANT: 0 violations under EVERY candidate weight** (T1-T5, local-as-study, both Review settings) — no weighting ever promotes a TFDA-indication (or local) doc into a **safety** query's cited top-5. Structural (TFDA-indication docs don't survive retrieval on safety queries; weighting only reorders retrieved docs). The v193 constitution holds under all candidate weights.
    - **KEY FINDING — local-tier does NOT change citations:** `local→top-5` and `local←top-5` = **0 under every tier**; top-5 set-change rate **constant 0.065 (2/31)** across T1-T5 (the 2 changes are non-local). Local-tier's ONLY effect is intra-pool **ordering** (inversions 13 at T1/T2 → 9 at T4 → 2 at T5). **The v198 "load-bearing" worry is DOWNGRADED: load-bearing on ORDERING, not on citations.**
    - **Review→tier matters MORE than local-tier:** Review=T2 shifts set-change 0.065→0.097 AND cuts intended label-above-study inversions 13→5 (narrative reviews wrongly compete with labels).
    - **RECOMMENDATION → KEEP local = Tier 2 ×1.5** (coherence: local IS cached FDA labeling that relabels to "FDA"; openFDA is T2; local must match its own source. Zero citation-membership cost either way; T2 maximizes the intended §2.10.3 label-above-study effect; 0 danger-path violations). **KEEP Review = Tier 4** (narrative ≠ systematic — systematic reviews already → T2 via most-specific-wins; Review=T2 is the wrong direction on both churn and label-authority). **Net: confirm both v198 defaults, now on 50-query evidence.** ⚠️ Coverage caveat: decision rests on 14 local-appearance pools; edge_ambiguous + some cjk_brand yielded empty pools — top up via more english_clinical/general_zh drug queries if more confidence wanted (signal is unambiguous within current coverage).
  - **✅ RATIFIED BY FOUNDER 2026-07-06 (on the 50-query sweep evidence above):** **local-corpus tier = Tier 2 ×1.5 (KEEP)** and **PubMed "Review" → Tier 4 (KEEP)** — both v198 defaults confirmed as-is. **Ratification is config-confirmation ONLY** — weighting remains SHADOW/log-only (`SOURCE_WEIGHT_SHADOW`, zero effect on user-visible ranking) until the weighting-activation slot ships; activation stays a separate future task (approved sequence: shadow ✅ v198 → Verify [P1] honest relabel ✅ v199 → weighting activation + DailyMed-as-5th-source).
  - **✅ ACTIVATED ON PROD 2026-07-06 (v200) — the tier decisions moved from "ratified, shadow" to LIVE:** the composite V1 (rerank × source_weight × tier_weight) with local = Tier 2 ×1.5 and Review = Tier 4 now reorders the real Research top_k behind `SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE` (activation seam = `source_weight_shadow.rank_by_composite_v1`, reused by `retriever.py` — no fork; PARITY-proven live==shadow). Code shipped OFF-by-default; **founder then set `SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE=true` in fly secrets and PASSED the §2.7 human-eye gate on prod 2026-07-06 → the tier weights are now LIVE.** Automated gates all passed pre-flip (parity 5/5 + live 0-fail; danger-path TFDA-cited 0 live; §2.7 18/2/0, both WARNs = known oscillators R10[reverted]/R20[documented safer-alt], zero reorder-caused regression); human-eye gate confirmed danger-path clean + indication positive-control (TFDA cited, v197 integrity intact) + OFF-vs-ON citation-set consistency (no churn). See STATE v200.

  - **⚠️ 2026-07-29 — THE RATIFICATION'S STATED PREMISE IS REFUTED BY MEASUREMENT. The decision is NOT reversed here; it is re-opened for the founder.** The recommendation above reads *"KEEP local = Tier 2 ×1.5 (**coherence: local IS cached FDA labeling** that relabels to 'FDA'; openFDA is T2; local must match its own source)"*. The fly-214 gate measured all 690 local documents: **100% carry ≤ 20 characters of real content (max 5, median 1); 190/190 `basic_info` docs are pure field labels with no values.** **`local` is not cached FDA labeling — it is drug-name-plus-section-heading boilerplate**, because the builder reads a data shape that never existed (root cause proven in code; the real ≈3.58M chars sit unread in `full_label`). See the **[P1] local drug corpus** entry at the top of this file.
    - **What this does and does not change.** It does **not** invalidate the sweep's *empirical* results — those measured actual behaviour and remain valid, and one of them (`local→top-5` = 0, `local←top-5` = 0 under **every** tier; top-5 set-change constant at 0.065) is now *explained* rather than merely observed: near-contentless documents cannot win or lose citation membership on content. It **does** invalidate the *reason given* for choosing T2 over a lower tier — "it must match openFDA because it is the same kind of thing" is false as built.
    - **Live effect today:** these stubs receive **Tier 2 ×1.5 → composite ×2.25**, i.e. they are boosted **above Tier-4 PubMed studies (×1.0)** for the intra-pool ordering that decides the `top_k=5` cut. Cross-ref the **[P2] Research pool budget** surface (11 whitelisted safety sections measured at composite rank 5 — one slot outside `top_k`).
    - **Recommendation: do NOT retier in isolation.** The tier question is downstream of the deprecate / repair / keep decision in the [P1] entry — repairing the corpus would make the original T2 reasoning *true*, and deprecating it makes the tier moot. **Decide the corpus first, then re-confirm or change the tier.** (Phase-3 recommends deprecation, which would make this config dead rather than wrong.)
    - **🔁 THIS IS THE FOURTH INSTANCE OF ONE CLASS: an instrument that is BLIND TO THE THING IT MEASURES.** Naming it so it stops being rediscovered:

      | 7 | **the DailyMed coverage metric** (2026-08-04) | counts only **whitelisted** sections, so a label whose warnings live in the un-whitelisted `34071-1` scores as *"no safety section"*. **The metric inherited the omission it should have exposed** — which is how "120/128 OTC-shaped" came to imply an OTC problem when **93% of the missing text is prescription**. Now CLAUDE.md **Rule 23** |

The generator had stopped emitting heading markers on 2026-06-10 (`3ad3ddc`); the parser still looked for them there, so the bar was slate regardless of what the text contained. **The measurement was correct and answered the wrong half of the pipeline.** Compare #13 (measured an arm that contributes nothing) — here we measured a value that never reaches the renderer |

      - *"0 hits above `min_score=0.6` for the whole DailyMed store"* (`c2_phase1b_measurement_20260804.md:214`) — ✅ **INDEPENDENTLY CONFIRMED.** The 2026-08-06 embedding reproduces it exactly: **0/4608**. The number is right.
      - *"aspirin's own doc never clears threshold"* — ⚠️ **TRUE OF THE RAW ARM, NOT ESTABLISHED OF THE STORE.** The probe generalised from the one arm that contributes nothing. DURLAZA (`ACETYLSALICYLIC ACID`, `34070-3`) scores **0.4031 / rank 500** on the raw arm; its rewritten-arm behaviour is **UNMEASURED**.
      - *"E-A is the only option that closes the Reye's gap → REFUTED, the aspirin doc is indexed and never retrieved (0/6)"* — the **REFUTED verdict stands** (it rests on end-to-end retrieval runs, not on the store probe), but its *"never retrieved"* rationale inherits the raw-arm limitation.
      - ⚠️ **A SECOND divergence in the same probe, recorded for completeness but NOT consequential here:** it used **`n_results=25`** against production's **5**. With 0 docs above the floor, 25 and 5 return the same empty set, so **this one changed no recorded conclusion.** It remains a latent defect for any query where >5 docs clear the floor. *(Self-refuted rather than reported as damage — Rule 21.)*
  - **📌 STANDING LESSON from instances 8–11 (all four produced by ONE work line, all four self-caught before reporting):**
    **STRING HEURISTICS OVER DRUG DATA ARE UNRELIABLE BY DEFAULT.** Drug labels legitimately name other
    drugs (cross-sensitivity), share vocabulary across species and routes, and mix container with dosage
    terms. **Every classification must be self-refuted before it is reported** — sample the positives
    *and* the negatives by hand, state the sample size, and record the rejected markers so they are not
    re-added. Where an exact key exists (`setid`, `moiety`, `doctype`), **use the key, not the text**.
    ❌ ~~*(Proposed as a standing practice for founder approval — CLAUDE.md NOT edited.)*~~ → ✅ **ADOPTED 2026-08-06 as CLAUDE.md Rule 21.** The rule adds the CHECK the proposal implied but did not state: *the report hand-samples both positives and negatives, states the sample size, and lists the rejected markers — a text-derived classification lacking all three is unverified.*
    - **📋 PROPOSED STANDING PRACTICE (founder approval requested — CLAUDE.md NOT edited):** *any ranking / weighting / tier ratification must inspect the **CONTENT** of a sample of what is being boosted or demoted, not only its rank position or citation membership.* A one-line addition to the sweep template: print N sampled documents' text alongside the rank deltas. **Cross-ref Rule 17** — *"a test that passes against hardcoded return values or stubbed calls without observing real effect is dead weight"*; a ranking sweep that never reads a document is the same failure at the measurement layer. ❌ ~~**This is a proposal, not an adopted rule.**~~ → ✅ **ADOPTED 2026-08-06 as CLAUDE.md Rule 22.** The rule adds the CHECK: *the sweep output contains document text; a sweep showing only ranks, IDs and citation membership has ratified nothing.*
  - **Flagged:** 2026-07-06 (v198 shadow ship); shadow is log-only so nothing is at risk until weighting is activated. **Sweep + recommendation: 2026-07-06 (v199 analysis slot). Ratified: 2026-07-06 (founder). Activated in code: 2026-07-06 (v200, shipped OFF). Flipped ON on prod + human-eye gate passed: 2026-07-06 (founder). Premise refuted by measurement, re-opened for founder: 2026-07-29 (fly-214 gate).**

- [OTHER] **[P3 · retrieval-recall — flagged at the v200 activation human-eye gate 2026-07-06] Indication-corpus recall depends on query phrasing**
  - **What:** the TFDA 核准適應症 corpus is retrieved only when the query carries strong signal words (台灣 / 核准 / 適應症). Weaker phrasings (e.g. 「{brand}適應症查詢」) miss the TFDA corpus — after the English query-rewrite the TFDA-corpus cosine similarity falls below the 0.6 threshold — and the answer falls back to FDA + PubMed with no TFDA citation.
  - **NOT weighting-related / NOT a regression:** confirmed 2026-07-06 to be **identical under `SOURCE_WEIGHT_ACTIVE` OFF and ON** (weighting only reorders what retrieval already returned; it cannot resurrect a doc that never cleared the 0.6 retrieval threshold). The strong-signal control (「冠脂妥台灣核准的適應症有哪些」) correctly cites TFDA under both. So this is a pre-existing retrieval-recall behavior, surfaced (not caused) by the activation gate.
  - **Not a bug (recall/relevance tradeoff), a candidate future improvement:** e.g. brand-name → TFDA-corpus routing (deterministic: a detected TFDA brand in the query forces a TFDA-corpus search), or a lower similarity threshold for TFDA-tagged/brand queries. Cross-ref the v195 `detect_brands_in_text` path (already deterministic brand detection at the endpoint) as a possible hook.
  - **🔴 NOW DEMONSTRATED ON PROD, not merely measured offline (2026-07-29, fly-214 human-eye gate).** The gate checklist's own TFDA rows produced **NO TFDA citation**, and the founder's retry with strong-signal phrasing retrieved it — a matched working/failing pair on live production:
    | phrasing | result |
    |---|---|
    | `冠脂妥適應症查詢` (weak signal) | **FDA 1 + PubMed 3 — no TFDA** |
    | `metformin 腎臟不好的病人可以用嗎` (no signal words) | **DailyMed 4 + PubMed 1 — no TFDA** |
    | **`冠脂妥台灣核准的適應症是什麼`** (strong signal: 台灣 + 核准 + 適應症) | ✅ **TFDA 核准適應症 1** — 冠脂妥膜衣錠5毫克, 許可證字號 衛署藥輸字第024597號 |
    This matches the 2026-07-06 offline measurement exactly (`冠脂妥適應症` cosine **0.5632**, below the 0.6 threshold). **The defect moves from "measured in a probe" to "reproduced in production by the founder".** Priority left at **[P3]** — the behaviour is unchanged and still a recall/relevance tradeoff, not a regression — but it is no longer theoretical.
  - **⚠️ OPS CONSEQUENCE (acted on):** a gate checklist that uses a weak-signal TFDA phrasing **fails its TFDA row for a reason that has nothing to do with the change under test** — which is what happened here and cost a retry round. **Canonical gate query, now named in the `[ops]` SOP: `冠脂妥台灣核准的適應症是什麼`.**
  - **Discovered:** 2026-07-06 (v200 activation human-eye gate). **Live prod confirmation:** 2026-07-29 (fly-214 gate) — see [`docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md`](docs/citation_gate_findings_20260729.md) Finding 2.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · verify grounding coverage — flagged at the Baton A prod gate 2026-07-09] openFDA-fallback interaction-truncation fix is unit-test-covered, NOT live-eyeballed**
  - **What:** Baton A (fly 204, `8a4db6f`) un-truncated the interaction/safety sections on BOTH sources (DailyMed `dailymed.py::_section_text` option-b + openFDA `fda.py::to_text` safety-sections-full). The **DailyMed half was live-verified** at the prod human-eye gate; the **openFDA-fallback half was NOT** — the fallback rarely triggers, and every common drug tried during the gate (incl. OTC aspirin / acetaminophen / ibuprofen) resolved via the DailyMed PRIMARY path, so a "goes-openFDA AND has a long interaction section" case couldn't be reproduced live.
  - **Coverage today:** `fda.py` safety-sections-full is client-level unit-tested (`tests/test_verify_dailymed.py::test_openfda_to_text_keeps_full_interaction_section`; warfarin's 6477-char openFDA interaction section confirmed fully retained). No live UI eyeball.
  - **Action:** re-verify live if a fallback long-section case appears in prod (a drug that misses DailyMed 34073-7 yet has a >2000-char openFDA interaction section). **Priority [P3]** — the unit coverage bounds the risk; the residual is only the missing live eyeball on a rare path.

- [HONESTY] **[P3 · verify honesty/consistency — surfaced at the Baton A prod gate 2026-07-09] Verify UI mixes 實證 / 循證 (both "evidence-based")**
  - **What:** the Verify title reads 「FDA 官方·實證醫學」 while the input label reads 「英文藥名查詢·循證」 — same meaning, but 循證 reads Mainland-style to zh-TW users; 實證 is the Taiwan-standard term.
  - **Fix-direction:** standardize to **實證** across the Verify UI copy. Frontend copy only (`utils/i18n-ui.ts` / verify labels), no logic. Batch with the next i18n copy pass.

- [OTHER] **[P3 · verify generation glitch — 1/5 Baton A prod-gate runs 2026-07-09] Occasional species hallucination in the interaction description**
  - **What:** on ONE warfarin+aspirin prod run, the description said 「獸醫師應評估…」 (veterinarian) for a human-drug query — a stray LLM generation artifact.
  - **NOT Baton-A-caused** (un-truncation adds label text; it cannot inject a "veterinarian" word) and **NOT reproduced** on the other 4 gate cases. Monitor frequency; revisit only if it recurs (it's an LLM-output artifact, not a code path). **1/5 gate runs.**

- [OTHER] **[P3 · verify SaMD verdict-phrasing — flagged at the Baton A2-typo prod gate 2026-07-13] Danger-path occasional SaMD verdict-phrasing (「不得併用」-class)**
  - **What:** the no-verdict constraint (`verify_system.md` — never assert "must not combine") is not 100%-stable on the strictest contraindicated pairs. During the A2-typo prod gate, tranylcypromine+fluoxetine (MAOI × SSRI, serotonin-syndrome danger-path) run ×4 produced **1/4** with a 「不得併用」-class verdict phrase; the other 3/4 held the present-and-cite / no-verdict line.
  - **NOT A2-typo-caused (pre-existing):** the indications un-truncation only ADDS descriptive label text; it cannot produce a "must not combine" verdict, and the 3/4-clean re-test rules it out. This is the same family as the Baton-A species-hallucination glitch above — a rare LLM-generation artifact on the no-verdict constraint, not a code path.
  - **Action:** monitor frequency; if it recurs, consider hardening `verify_system.md`'s no-verdict wording (would need a §2.7 ExplainJudge/VerifyJudge re-baseline per Rule 17) or adding a danger-path verdict-phrasing check to §2.7. **1/4 gate runs.**
  - **Discovered:** 2026-07-13 (Baton A2-typo prod human-eye gate).

- [DONE] **[P3 · DailyMed corpus (dormant, B-2 Phase 1) — cross-market salt-choice coverage gap, accepted-as-is 2026-07-14]**
  - **What:** the DailyMed corpus backbone is the TFDA mono moieties, whose SALT choice can differ from the US mono label's salt. When TW uses one salt and the US mono label is filed under a DIFFERENT salt, the drug has NO DailyMed mono in this corpus. Concrete: **`PERINDOPRIL ARGININE`** (TW) drops as US-combo-only (Prestalia = perindopril arginine + amlodipine); the US mono is **perindopril *erbumine*** (Aceon), a different salt not in the backbone → perindopril has no DailyMed mono doc here. Same class as the head-word rescue misses (`EPINEPHRINE HCL`→lidocaine+epi combo where `EPINEPHRINE`→EpiPen is a separate string; `COLISTIN`→colistimethate under a different name).
  - **NOT a bug (safe failure):** the drug is **absent** (dropped/missed), NOT mis-grounded — the mono-preference drop rule guarantees a mono moiety never grounds on a combo. Absence is safe; TFDA still covers the drug for TW.
  - **Fix-direction (deferred):** a base-INN / salt-synonym map (moiety → base INN → all US salt forms) would close both this and the head-word rescue misses in one pass. Defer to a future synonym-map pass; not worth a rebuild now.
  - **Discovered:** 2026-07-14 (B-2 Phase 1 corpus eyeball).

- [DONE] **[P3 · DailyMed corpus (dormant, B-2 Phase 1) — a few low-value homeopathic mono docs, accepted-as-is 2026-07-14]**
  - **What:** a handful of mono-rescues land on single-ingredient **homeopathic** labels for moieties with no real US Rx mono: **`ALUMINUM OXIDE`→Alumina, `AMMONIUM CHLORIDE`→Ammonium muriaticum, `CENTELLA ASIATICA`→Hydrocotyle**. Technically mono (1 active moiety) so kept, but clinically low-value.
  - **Harmless / accepted:** these drugs are rarely queried; the Phase-2 danger-path gate covers any safety-misread risk at wiring time; dropping them is not worth a rebuild. Recorded for awareness only.
  - **Discovered:** 2026-07-14 (B-2 Phase 1 corpus eyeball).
